I'm also tracking t10_a060's progress—it's currently at step 4235 out of 5000 on gen_2, running at about 2.6 iterations per second, so it should finish this generation in roughly 5 more minutes.

Once gen_2 training wraps up, there's evaluation and data generation to run through, then gen_3 begins.     "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

I need to verify a few key things after the batch restart: whether t15_a050 is actually running on GPU 1, if t15_a060 got restarted as expected, and what the batch worker actually did. ```text
Now I have the full process list on westd-17855. Let me map it:

1. **PID 382885** (tmux): `interv_react_s43` — 410M intervention experiment, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0, GPU 0
2. **PID 577236/577241**: `t25_a045_s43_fp32` — 410M cross-seed, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=5, GPU 5
3. **PID 600997/601002**: `t20_a075_s43_fp32` — 410M cross-seed, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=4, GPU 4


4. **PID 655757/655764**: `t10_a075_s42_fp32_14b` — 1.4B resume, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=2, GPU 2
5. **PID 655834/655841**: `t10_a060_s42_fp32_14b` — 1.4B resume, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3, GPU 3
6. **PID 661770/661775**: `t10_a050_s42_fp32_14b` — 1.4B fresh start, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0

So I've got three 410M experiments running on GPUs 0, 4, and 5, then three 1.4B runs on GPUs 2, 3, and 0 — two resuming from checkpoints and one starting fresh.

The batch restart worker then started `t15_a050_14b` on GPU 1 with `--start_gen 2`, but didn't restart `t15_a060` after it was killed and never started `t15_a075` at all. Now GPU 0 is overloaded with three processes: the 410M experiment, the fresh `t10_a050_14b`, and something else competing for resources.

The real issue is that the worker chose to run `t10_a050` from scratch instead of resuming `t15_a060` which already had generations 0-2 completed, or starting `t15_a075` — this is clearly wasteful. I flagged this in Report #67, but the batch restart was probably already in motion when I sent it, so the Director may not have had time to intervene.

Now I'm checking whether any new generation 2 metrics have appeared in the MAUVE status to see if there's been any progress since then.

[System 触发审查]:
**R8 审稿报告处理——请独立审视我的 Action Matrix 可行性和遗漏**

Overall: 3.0 (Findings)。4 个 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE + 17 条逐条 action。请逐条审视方案是否可行、是否有更好的替代方案、是否有遗漏。

---

## Holistic Pass: 4 个系统性问题

**[SYSTEMIC_ISSUE A] 统计声明强度超出数据支撑**（W-C1 + W-C3 + W-m1）
FPR CI [0%,41%] 隐藏在附录 + power≈37% 无法区分 onset vs artifact + seed 不确定性低报。
→ 核心 claim 不受影响（cascade ordering 14/15、method comparison κ=0.185 独立于 FPR CI），但 PermG "zero FPR" 的独特性声明需要降级。

**[SYSTEMIC_ISSUE B] Detection boundary 验证不足**（W-C2 + Q2 + Q3）
2T 拟合 2 参数。但现在有 20/20 Phase Diagram（5 个 T 值），可升级为 5T LOO-CV，彻底解决零自由度问题。这是最大的突破口。

**[SYSTEMIC_ISSUE C] 文献处理不严谨**（W-C4 + W-M5 + Missing Refs）
Du & Tanaka-Ishii 三重错误 + Schaeffer 映射缺失 + 4 篇缺失引用。纯 paper 修复。

**[SYSTEMIC_ISSUE D] 正文信息密度失衡**（W-M4 + Visual）
Discussion 100词太短 + pages 4-5 纯文本 + 仅 4 display items。需要重组结构。

---

## Per-Comment Action Matrix

### CRITICAL

**R8-C1. W-C1: FPR CI [0%,41%] 低报**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：(1) 主文 §4.1 和 Abstract 改为 "FPR=0% (cluster-adjusted 95% CI: [0%, 41%], Appendix R)"。(2) MMCT 权重重算：在 FPR=10% 假设下 PermG 的 w 是否仍最高？如果 w 下降但 rank 不变，核心结论不变。(3) 如果 MMCT rank 翻转，需要重写 §4.1 结论。
- 负责：Paper Agent（改文字）+ Agent（MMCT 重算脚本）
- ETA：1d
- **请 Reviewer 评估**：在 FPR=10% 假设下 MMCT 权重是否翻转？我记得 PermG 的优势不仅是 FPR=0%，还有 directional specificity——即使 FPR 升到 10%，w 可能仍最高。

**R8-C2. W-C2: Detection boundary 零自由度**
- 类别：补实验（重新分析，不需 GPU）
- 方案：**利用 20/20 Phase Diagram 的 5 个 T 值做 LOO-CV**。训练: 4 个 T（如去掉 T=25），验证: 剩余 1 个 T。5-fold LOO 产出 5 个 MAE + coverage 率。这直接解决 "2T fit 2 params" 的质疑——用 5T 数据 LOO 验证，effective DOF > 0。
  - 具体：修改 verify_theory_d102.py，输入 20/20 grid 全部数据点，做 leave-one-T-out CV
  - 还可以加 AIC/BIC 与替代模型（linear、quadratic、exponential）的比较
  - β bootstrap CI：从 20 个数据点做 1000 次 bootstrap resample
- 负责：Agent（脚本 + 分析）
- ETA：0.5d（纯计算，无需 GPU）
- **请 Reviewer 评估**：5T LOO-CV 是否足够回应这条 CRITICAL？还是需要额外的 T 值实验数据（如 T=8, T=12）？

**R8-C3. W-C3: Power artifact vs true onset**
- 类别：补分析 + 改 paper
- 方案：(1) 论文明确区分 "detection power boundary"（方法检测下界）和 "signal onset"（信号出现位置）。承认观测到的是 detection power boundary，但 cross-T consistency（5 个 T 值的 onset 位置系统性左移）支持底层存在 real phase transition。(2) 模型比较：smooth power curve vs step-function model，用 AIC/BIC 区分。(3) Phase diagram 本身就是最好的证据——如果只是 power artifact，不同 T 的 onset 位置不应该系统性遵循 α_c(T) ∝ T^{-γ} 的 scaling law。
- 负责：Agent（模型比较分析）+ Paper Agent（§5.2 改写）
- ETA：1d
- **请 Reviewer 评估**：cross-T scaling law 的一致性是否足以回应 power artifact 质疑？或者需要做 simulation（在 no-transition null 下模拟 detection pattern）？

**R8-C4. W-C4: Du & Tanaka-Ishii 引用三重错误**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：(1) 修正 arXiv ID 和标题。(2) 重新定位——Du & Tanaka-Ishii 研究 mode collapse（生成时重复），非 model collapse（迭代退化）。从 "closest predecessor" 降级为 "related but distinct phenomenon"。(3) 检查 bib 文件与 PDF 编译版本一致性。
- 负责：Paper Agent
- ETA：0.5d
- **请 Reviewer 评估**：是否需要 WebSearch 确认 Du & Tanaka-Ishii 的真实内容？

**R8-C5. W-C5: 贡献定位重校**
- 类别：改 paper（framing）
- 方案：(1) 贡献 1："landscape characterization" → "systematic empirical characterization"。(2) 贡献 2：Proposition 1 保留但降低语气——"formalized observation" 而非暗示理论突破。Proposition 3 降级为 "empirical scaling observation"。(3) 贡献 3："method sensitivity" → "引入已知统计最佳实践到 NLP model collapse 语境"，承认对计量经济学不新，但对 NLP 社区有增量价值。(4) 整体定位为 "empirical study applying established econometric tools to model collapse detection"。
- 负责：Paper Agent（Intro + Abstract 改写）
- ETA：1d

### MAJOR

**R8-M1. W-M1: Cross-architecture 循环论证**
- 类别：补分析 + 改 paper
- 方案：定义独立的 coupling 判定标准——entropy-diversity 时间序列的 Pearson r > 0.8 作为 a priori 条件。回溯验证：15 个 model-seed 组合中，哪些满足 r > 0.8？如果满足 r > 0.8 的恰好是 10 个 confirming 的，则循环论证成立；如果有 r > 0.8 但 non-confirming 的，说明不是循环。
- 负责：Agent（Pearson r 计算）
- ETA：0.5d（已有数据，纯分析）

**R8-M2. W-M2: Intervention n=2 不足**
- 类别：改 paper + 可能补实验
- 方案：(1) 在论文中标注 "proof-of-concept / anecdotal evidence"，不作为核心 claim。(2) 如果有 GPU 时间，加 2-3 个 α 值（α=0.50, 0.75）× seed=42 的 intervention 实验。每个约 2h GPU。(3) 但如果 GPU 紧张（1.4B 优先），可以只改 framing。
- 负责：Agent（如果补实验）+ Paper Agent（改 framing）
- ETA：1-2d
- **请 Reviewer 评估**：在 DDL 前 GPU 资源有限的情况下，intervention 补实验 vs 只改 framing，哪个 ROI 更高？

**R8-M3. W-M3: fp32/bf16 sync-dip 翻转**
- 类别：写 rebuttal + 改 paper
- 方案：C2 已经在 R7→R8 之间从 "mechanism claim" 重写为 "empirical observation of T-dependent transient dissolution"。不再 claim precision mechanism。论文已经诚实报告了这个现象并标注为 open question。rebuttal 论点：(1) fp32 是全部实验的标准精度，bf16 仅在早期探索中使用过；(2) 翻转本身支持 detection 对 training setup 的敏感性——这是 Limitation 而非 validity 问题。
- 负责：Director（rebuttal）+ Paper Agent（确认 C2 叙事）
- ETA：0.5d

**R8-M4. W-M4: Discussion 过短**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：扩展 §6 至 0.5 页（约 300-400 词），覆盖：(1) 5/15 non-confirming 原因分析；(2) ML pipeline 实际指导意义（何时、如何部署 canary monitoring）；(3) detection boundary ≠ signal onset 的含义和 open questions。
- 负责：Paper Agent
- ETA：0.5d
- 注意：主文 8 页限制，需从其他 section 压缩出空间

**R8-M5. W-M5: Schaeffer et al. 映射**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：§2 扩展 2-3 句，明确本文的 model collapse 操作性定义对应 Schaeffer 8 种分类中的 "iterative training degradation"（Type 2 或类似）。
- 负责：Paper Agent（需先 WebSearch 确认 Schaeffer 分类细节）
- ETA：0.5d

**R8-M6. W-M6: "Pitfalls" → "Design considerations"**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：全文替换 "methodological pitfalls" 为 "design considerations"。
- 负责：Paper Agent
- ETA：trivial

### MINOR

**R8-m1. W-m1: Seed sensitivity 定量化**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：在 §5.2 或 Appendix 中报告 α_c 的 seed-adjusted uncertainty。T=10 α=0.50: s42=6/8 vs s43=0/8，真实 CI 远大于 SE=0.006。改为报告 inter-seed range。
- 负责：Paper Agent
- ETA：0.5d

**R8-m2. W-m2: 13 vs 12 contamination levels 统一**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：全文统一为 "13 contamination levels (including null control)"。
- 负责：Paper Agent
- ETA：trivial

**R8-m3. W-m3: Figure 字体偏小**
- 类别：改 paper
- 方案：增大 Figure 1 和 Figure 2 的 axis label、legend、tick label 字号。
- 负责：Agent（修改绘图脚本）
- ETA：0.5d

### Missing References

**R8-Ref1-4**: 补充 4 篇引用
- Tank et al. (2021) — 非线性 Granger causality，说明选择 PermG 的理由
- arXiv:2501.08958 — KAN-based Granger
- arXiv:2509.16499 — Model collapse generalization perspective
- Schick et al. (2024) — Self-play fine-tuning
- 负责：Paper Agent（WebSearch 验证后添加）
- ETA：0.5d

### Questions 回应思路

- Q1 (MMCT FPR=10%): 需要 Agent 重算
- Q2 (β bootstrap CI): 包含在 R8-C2 方案中
- Q3 (Power artifact): 包含在 R8-C3 方案中
- Q4 (Independent coupling metric): 包含在 R8-M1 方案中
- Q5 (Du & Tanaka-Ishii): 包含在 R8-C4 方案中
- Q6 (Schaeffer mapping): 包含在 R8-M5 方案中

1. **R8-C2 是最大突破口**：20/20 Phase Diagram 给了我们 5 个 T 值，可以做 5T LOO-CV 彻底解决零自由度。这个方案的技术可行性如何？有没有统计学上的陷阱？

Excellent! Now I have comprehensive information. Let me compile the findings:

## Summary Report

### 1. **P_max Grid Search Raw Data (theory_verification_d102_pmax_grid.json)**

**Key Statistics:**
- **Best model**: P_max = 0.75, β = 0.05, A = 6.434, z₀ = 20.0
- **Fit quality**: test MAE = 8.31e-06 (excellent), McFadden R² = 0.372
- **Cross-validation**: 6/6 points (100% CI coverage), MAE = 8.31e-06
- **P_max grid resolution**: 11 values [0.5 → 1.0, step 0.05]
- **β grid resolution**: 46 values [0.05 → 0.50, step 0.01]

**Data Points:**
- Training: 14 points (T={10,20}, α ∈ {0.0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.3, 0.35, 0.4, 0.45, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0})
- Validation: 6 points (T={5,15}, α ∈ {0.45, 0.5, 0.75})
- Extra: 4 points (T=25, α ∈ {0.45, 0.5, 0.6, 0.75})
- **Total: 24 unique (T, α) combinations tested**

**α_c values (critical contamination levels):**
- T=5: α_c ≈ 99.0 (ceiling)
- T=10: α_c ≈ 1.087
- T=15: α_c ≈ 0.0189
- T=20: α_c ≈ 0.00106
- T=25: α_c ≈ 0.000114
- T=50: α_c ≈ 1.11e-07

---

### 2. **Related Theory Verification Files**

| File | Purpose | Key Content |
|------|---------|-------------|
| **theory_verification.json** | Power-law α_c(T) model | theory_offset_power: R²=0.9999, α_∞=0.193, γ=2.408 |
| **theory_verification_d102.json** | Cross-T prediction validation | Model: P(det) = Φ(A·max(0,α-α_∞)^β·√T - z₀), validated across T={5,10,15,20} |
| **t20_power_theory_verification.md** | T=20 predict-then-verify report | Locked predictions before T=20 runs; 4/5 PASS (80%); α_c left-shift confirmed |
| **verify_theory_d102_pmax_grid.py, verify_theory_d102.py, verify_theory_d102_pmax.py** | Analysis scripts | Multi-model comparison scripts |

---

### 3. **Paper Framing (paper_framing.md)**

**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 Main Track (ACL series template, 8 pages + unlimited appendix)

**Narrative Arc** (D130 Discovery Paper Reframe):
- **Headline**: A Detection Phase Transition in Iterative Model Collapse
- **Core Discovery**: Detection phase transition at α_c ≈ 0.476 (sigmoid R²=0.969, k≥200)
  - Threshold is **per-generation mixing ratio (α)**, NOT cumulative contamination
  - Transition steepness is T-dependent (predicted by Power Theory)
  - Empirical transition width Δα: median=0.022, CI=[0.008, 0.060]

**Four Core Claims (Reorganized D130):**
1. **C1: Detection Phase Transition** (核心发现)
   - PermG onset at α≈0.476 (T=10), sigmoid R²=0.969
   - Fisher exact p=3.45×10⁻¹¹ for model-free jump evidence
   - Four-layer taxonomy: structure (α≥0.10) → canary instability (α≥0.25) → causal detection (α≥0.50) → cascade (α≥0.75)
   - α=0.60: sync dip (non-monotonic dose-response)

2. **C2: S(α)×R(α) Competition Model** (机制解释)
   - Signal strength S monotonically increases with α
   - Temporal resolution R non-monotonic, drops at α≈0.60 (synchronization dip)
   - Predicts 4 regimes validated by PermG dose-response
   - √T power scaling with γ=2.408 (theory R²=0.999)

3. **C3: Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT)** (发现工具)
   - 5 methods: naive_fwd, diff_fwd, threshold_onset, PermG, TY
   - PermG null FPR = 0/24 (only zero-FPR method)
   - K≥3 consensus in 10/14 architecture-seed combinations
   - Method sensitivity: naive 100% sig → differenced PermG 25% sig

4. **C4: Practical Applications**
   - Early cessation: 58.3% perplexity reduction (CI: [28.5%, 63.7%])
   - Data strategy: accumulate mode defeats detection; replace mode works
   - bf16 precision eliminates causal signal

**Key Constraints (Updated D181 Reviewer Report 46):**
- Onset boundary: sharp transition [0.45, 0.50], not step function
- α_c position uncertainty (CI=[0.425, 0.558]) ≠ transition gradient (Δα≈0.02)
- α_c(T) √T scaling: calibrated at T=10, consistency checks at T=5,15,20
- Sync dip α=0.60: T-invariant structural regime (not power artifact)
- 6/8 structural ceiling: mauve metric insensitivity (not method limitation)

**Current Status:**
- T=10 complete (13-point α-sweep: α ∈ {0.0, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.30, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00})
- T=20 verification: 4/5 PASS, α=0.45 failed point prediction but within CI
- T=25: 4 validation points
- Architecture coverage: 14+ combinations (Pythia-6.9B/1.4B/410M, GPT-2, OPT, Qwen, SmolLM3, C4)

---

### **Key Data Points Summary**

| Aspect | Coverage |
|--------|----------|
| **T values** | T={5,10,15,20,25,50} (6 T values tested; primary focus T=10,20) |
| **α values** | 13 primary points [0.0, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.30, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00] + finer grid |
| **Grid coverage** | 11×46 P_max×β grid (506 combinations) for profile likelihood |
| **Seed robustness** | 4-5 seeds per condition (s42, s43, s44, etc.) |
| **Architectures** | 14+ model/dataset combinations |
| **Methods** | 5-method MMCT framework |

---

**Critical Insight**: The α_c theory is exceptionally well-characterized—the P_max multiplicative ceiling model achieves test MAE of 8.3e-06 on validation data, and the power-law α_c(T) scaling exhibits R²=0.9999. The transition is confirmed as sharp (Δα≈0.022) but its position is uncertain due to sparse α-grid. T=20 data largely validates the √T power scaling theory, confirming non-monotonic dose-response and α_c left-shift.

Perfect! I now have comprehensive information from all three files. Let me compile the key findings:

## Key Contents Summary: distrib_canary_collapse Project

### Deadline & Timeline
- **Target Submission**: EMNLP 2026 (ARR June), with deadline approximately **2026-06-15**
- **Current Status** (as of 2026-05-22): Phase Diagram 18-20 cells complete; 1.4B scale verification in progress
- **Paper Status**: R8 fresh review submitted on 2026-05-22 (review ID: apr_20260522T094342_2q1d)

---

### What's Remaining (Top Priority Tasks)
1. **Phase Diagram completion** (2/2 remaining cells):
   - t25_a050 (predicted 6/8) — ~5h running
   - t25_a060 ⭐ (critical sync-dip dissolution test) — ~5h running
   
2. **1.4B Scale Verification** (3/6 running, 4/6 recovering from failures):
   - 2 experiments actively running (t10_a060, t10_a075 with gen2+ fixes)
   - 4 experiments restarting due to prior SIGTERM/failure (t10_a050, t15_a050, t15_a060, t15_a045 preparing)
   
3. **Paper Revisions** (R7 feedback implementation):
   - Fix R²=1.000 overclaim (report R²=0.883 instead)
   - Reframe "first-principles" as "parametric explanation"
   - Clarify 5 vs 6 families/13 levels ambiguity
   - Compress Limitations to 3 points

---

### Key Decisions on Power Theory & Phase Diagram

**D165 (α_c Closed-Form Theory)**: Confirmed power law for detectability threshold:
- **Formula**: α_c(T) = α_∞ + A·T^(-1/(2β)) 
- **Parameters**: β=0.208, γ=2.408, α_∞=0.193 (detection lower bound)
- **Evidence**: 7-point fit with R²=1.000 on empirical data; T=5 predicts α_c≥1.0 (undetectable)
- **Status**: Proposition 2 written; T=5 experiments validating the prediction (all 0/8 confirmed via D173)

**D177 (Claim Structure)**: Phase Diagram landscape supports three layers:
- **C1** (40% weight): 2D structural pattern — blind zone below α≈0.48, detectability floor ceiling at 6/8
- **C2** (30% weight): α_c left-shift with T (Scenario A confirmed D188) — sync-dip is T-dependent transient, dissolving at T=25 (5/8)
- **C3** (20% weight): Multi-method necessity — PermG primary (0% FPR), other methods fill detection gaps
- **C4** (10% weight): Prevention paradox from intervention analysis

**D182-D188 (Phase Diagram Status)**: 
- 18/20 cells complete; final 2 (t25_a050, t25_a060) running
- **Confirmed phenomena**:
  - T=5: Full blackout (0/8 across all α) — validates α_c(5)≥1.0 theory
  - T=10/15/20: Robust sync-dip at α≈0.60 (0/8 PermG, 6/8 robust pairs with other methods)
  - T=25: Sync-dip dissolution begins (5/8 at α=0.60) — supports transient interpretation
  - α_c progression: T=10: [0.46,0.52] → T=15: <0.45 → T=20/25: ~0.23 (left-shift confirmed)

**D185-D187 (Sync-Dip Mechanism)**: 
- Rejected: S×R resonance hypothesis (no α-specific lag structure, peak_lag=-1 everywhere)
- Confirmed: MAUVE floor saturation (mean=0.029, 100% observations <0.05) explains PermG failure
- Track D Transfer Entropy signal confirmed as shared-trend artifact post-detrending

---

### D189: Submission Strategy (Latest Decision 2026-05-22 09:47 UTC)
- **Core submission**: 410M Pythia model (sufficient for EMNLP, predicted 3.5-4.0/5.0 score)

- **Language constraint**: Cannot claim "scale-invariant" with only 2 scale points; use "consistent across 410M and 1.4B"
- **Do not delay** for 1.4B completion

---

### Current Resource Status

- **Running experiments**: 3 main Phase Diagram cells (t25_a050, t25_a060 + cross-seed validations)
- **1.4B verification**: 2 running, 4 in restart/preparation pipeline

```text
Now I have comprehensive context. Let me analyze the Action Matrix systematically.

Key facts from the data:
1. **Phase Diagram**: 20/20 complete (5 T values × 4 α values). Plus the full T=10 13-point α-sweep.
2. **α_c theory data**: 24 unique (T, α) data points total. But the theory was fit on T={10,20} and validated on T={5,15} with T=25 extra.
3. **P_max model**: P_max=0.75 fixed, β=0.24 fixed, 2 free params. 
4. **Model validation**: Test MAE=0.000 on the validation set (T=5,15), though T=25 shows MAE=0.031.
5. **Deadline**: ~June 15, 2026 (ARR June). ~23 days from now.
6. **Current claim structure**: C1 (phase transition 40%), C2 (sync-dip/mechanism 30%), C3 (MMCT 20%), C4 (intervention 10%).

For R8-C1, the Director wants me to report the FPR confidence interval in text and then recalculate the MMCT weight accordingly.

The core tension is whether PermG's ranking holds up when FPR moves from 0% to 10%. The method's directional specificity (forward-only detection) should preserve its relative advantage even with higher FPR, but the confidence interval [0%, 41%] from 24 null tests is quite wide. The real problem is that "zero FPR" was the key selling point, so downgrading to a range with 41% as the upper bound weakens that narrative.

The backup strategy is solid: if PermG still ranks lowest-FPR among the compared methods under the FPR=10% assumption, the claim shifts from "uniquely zero-FPR" to "best-in-class FPR." This is computationally straightforward, though I should verify what cluster adjustment actually does to the confidence interval—if clusters are defined by model, seed, and T, the effective sample size could be much smaller than 24.

The 5-fold leave-one-out cross-validation on the 20/20 Phase Diagram data is the real opportunity here, but there are statistical complications with how the grid structure affects the analysis. The step-function nature of the data—mostly 0/8 or 6/8 outcomes—means the model is really just learning where that transition happens. A better validation approach would be leave-one-T-out cross-validation across all 5 temperature values, tracking prediction error and confidence interval coverage per fold, and comparing against simpler baselines like constant or linear models. The current setup with P_max=0.75 and β=0.24 fixed avoids the degeneracy problem, but I need to make sure the LOO-CV uses this same specification consistently throughout.

With 5T leave-one-T-out CV, each fold trains on 4 temperature values (16 data points total) to predict the held-out temperature—that's 14 effective degrees of freedom against 2 free parameters, which directly addresses the reviewer's concern about overfitting. The real question is whether the model can actually predict the critical α_c for an unseen temperature value, since all five temperatures follow the same √T scaling by construction. The Director is also asking whether we need additional temperatures like T=8 or T=12 to strengthen the validation.

Given that our current set {5,10,15,20,25} spans a 5× range in temperature, the coverage is solid enough. Adding intermediate values would help, but if the LOO-CV shows consistent predictions across the held-out folds, we can make a strong case without them. My recommendation is to stick with the 5T approach but pair it with model comparison using AIC/BIC, bootstrap confidence intervals for β, and explicit acknowledgment that β is fixed rather than fitted—which is actually a strength since it reduces the parameter count and overfitting risk.

The trickiest part is distinguishing whether the scaling behavior reflects a genuine phase transition or just a power-law artifact. The Director's strategy of using cross-temperature consistency plus model comparison should help settle this.

If the critical exponent α_c follows a clean T^{-γ} scaling law across temperatures, that's harder to dismiss as pure power artifact—a true power-artifact hypothesis would predict detection onset based on sample size and test power, but not necessarily a systematic scaling relationship. However, the counter-argument is equally compelling: if the signal grows smoothly with α and the PermG test power increases with T, then the observed onset would naturally shift leftward with larger T, mimicking a phase transition through the lens of statistical power alone. The scaling law might just be reflecting how test power grows with sample size.

To really settle this, I'd want to run a formal simulation under the null hypothesis to see whether the observed scaling pattern emerges from power effects alone. Python) — just generate synthetic data under the null hypothesis, apply the same detection method, and see if the artifact emerges. This would be a clean way to either validate or refute the power concern without needing months of theoretical work.

For the Du & Tanaka-Ishii citation, the Director should verify those three errors (wrong arXiv ID, title mismatch, mischaracterization) through WebSearch, and honestly, if one reference has that many mistakes, a broader audit of other citations makes sense too.

On the contribution repositioning, I agree the language needs toning down, but I want to make sure we don't oversell or undersell — the real novelty here is discovering the phase transition pattern itself, not explaining it theoretically. "Systematic empirical characterization" works for C1, though we need to be careful the paper still stakes a genuine claim.

For the circular reasoning concern with coupling, defining Pearson r > 0.8 a priori and then checking it retrospectively is a solid move, but that threshold still feels somewhat arbitrary. Testing robustness across multiple thresholds (0.6, 0.7, 0.8, 0.9) would be stronger than relying on a single cutoff.

On the intervention experiments with only n=2 and tight GPU constraints, downgrading the framing to "proof-of-concept" makes more sense than trying to squeeze out more runs in the next three weeks.

The fp32/bf16 discrepancy is fine to position as a limitation since all reported results use fp32 anyway.

The Discussion section definitely needs expansion to 300-400 words, but that means something else has to give—probably Related Work or pushing method details to the Appendix. The Director should figure out what to trim before adding content.

For the Schaeffer mapping, I just need 2-3 sentences in the methods section and should verify the taxonomy details with a quick search. The "Pitfalls" → "Design considerations" rename is just a find-replace, no substance there. The minor items are all straightforward.

I'm also tracking 4 missing references that need WebSearch verification.

On the 5T LOO-CV feasibility question—this is statistically sound as long as β stays fixed across all folds and P_max=0.75 is treated as a fixed empirical constant rather than something being re-fit. That means each fold only optimizes A and z₀ on 16 training points, which is well-conditioned. I should report both MAE and calibration metrics to be thorough.

There's one important caveat though: T=5 is the "all zero" condition where every measurement is 0/8, so leaving it out of training removes essentially no information about the transition dynamics. When it appears in a test fold, any reasonable model will predict near-zero anyway, making that fold almost trivially correct. So the real number of informative folds is closer to 4 than 5—the Director should flag this limitation.

For the power artifact question, I'm thinking through a bootstrap simulation approach: generate 1000 synthetic datasets under the null hypothesis where the true signal is just linear scaling with no phase transition, apply the permutation test to each one, then check whether the apparent onsets follow the T^{-γ} pattern. If they don't, that supports a genuine phase transition; if they do, the pattern might just be an artifact of how the test scales across T values. This is computationally cheap to run.

On sequencing, I'd push back slightly on the Director's ordering. C2 and C3 should really run together since they're both attacking the same core question—is the phase boundary real or an artifact? Meanwhile C1, C5, and C4 are mostly writing work that can happen in parallel. The Discussion should then wait for C2 and C3 results so it can actually discuss what those analyses found.

For the 3.0 to 3.5 jump, the 5T LOO-CV plus power simulation directly addresses what the reviewer asked for. The framing downgrade helps by tightening the gap between claims and evidence, and expanding the Discussion shows more rigor. But there's a real risk here—if the power simulation reveals that the scaling law could emerge from power artifacts alone, the whole phase transition story falls apart. The Director needs to be ready for that possibility. Getting 1.4B results would also be valuable if they're available, since showing consistency across scales is worth meaningful points.

Looking at what's missing from the action plan: we need an explicit null simulation for C3 rather than just mentioning it as a possibility. The rebuttal letter itself isn't addressed—ARR requires a formal response explaining the changes. There's also a gap between the seed sensitivity analysis and the α_c theory—the LOO-CV needs to account for how detection rates vary across seeds, which affects the uncertainty in α_c estimates. And the new T=25 α=0.60 data showing 5/8 detections contradicts the sync-dip hypothesis, which needs to be incorporated into the narrative.

Actually, I see now that C2 is fundamentally about the S(α)×R(α) competition model with the sync-dip explanation, but since the sync-dip appears transient and dissolves at higher T values, the action matrix should address this T-dependent behavior explicitly rather than treating it as a separate issue. The bigger consistency problem is that we have two versions of the α_c theory—one with P_max implicitly 1, one with P_max=0.75—and the LOO-CV needs to use the newer P_max model while the paper still reports results from the older version.

Now I have enough context. Let me now compose a comprehensive response to the Director's request.

Key data points for the α_c theory:
- Training set: T={10,20}, 14 points
- Validation: T={5,15}, 6 points
- Extra: T=25, 4 points
- Total: 24 unique (T, α) data points

For the 20/20 Phase Diagram, the data is:
- 5 T values × 4 α values = 20 cells
- Each cell has k/8 detection count (PermG forward FDR)


- LOO-CV would train on 16 points and test on 4, with only A and z₀ as free parameters since P_max and β are fixed.

The tension here is that the 20-cell grid only samples 4 α values, which might not capture the full resolution from the T=10 sweep that has 13 α values, so I need to decide whether to use the coarser grid or incorporate the finer α resolution.

Looking at the specific folds: T=5 is trivial since all cells are 0/8, T=10 contains the interesting transition region with mixed outcomes like 6/8, and I should examine what T=20 contributes to understand the full picture. I'm noticing the α=0.60 condition shows an anomalous pattern—it dips to 0/8 at T=10, 15, and 20, then jumps to 5/8 at T=25—which doesn't align with what the simple model predicts and could be problematic for the cross-validation analysis. be outliers in the leave-one-out cross-validation. The Director has three options: exclude the α=0.60 points entirely and treat the sync-dip as a separate phenomenon already handled in the paper's C2 analysis, include them and accept the performance hit, or extend the model with an explicit sync-dip term. Since the paper already addresses sync-dip separately, removing α=0.60 from the α_c validation is justified. Now I need to structure my response with feasibility assessments for each item, flag any gaps, and address the four judgment questions.

sig 的非对称性）。即使假设 FPR=10%，如果其他方法 FPR 同等或更高，PermG 相对排名不变。建议：MMCT 重算时同时报告所有方法在 FPR=10% 假设下的 w 值变化，展示排名稳定性（sensitivity analysis），比单点重算更有说服力。\n\n**⚠️ 遗漏**：CI=[0%,41%] 来自 Clopper-Pearson n=24。但如果 null 实验之间存在 cluster 相关性（同 model-seed 系列），有效 n 可能远小于 24。需要明确 cluster-adjusted CI 的计算方式和实际值——如果有效 n=6（按 model-seed 聚类），CI 可能宽到 [0%, 63%]，影响更大。\n\n---\n\n**R8-C2 (5T LOO-CV)** ← 最关键，详细展开\n[severity: warning] **方案基本可行但有三个统计陷阱：**\n\n**陷阱 1：T=5 fold 是无信息的**。T=5 全部 0/8，任何合理模型都能正确预测 ≈0。这个 fold 不提供区分力。有效 fold 只有 4 个，不是 5 个。论文中必须承认这一点。\n\n**陷阱 2：α=0.60 sync-dip 破坏模型假设**。P_max 模型假设 P(det) 对 α 单调递增。但 α=0.60 在 T=10/15/20 表现为 0/8（低于 α=0.50 的 6/8），违反单调性。如果 α=0.60 纳入 LOO-CV，它会成为系统性离群点，拉高 MAE。**建议**：LOO-CV 分两版：(a) 排除 α=0.60（理由：sync-dip 已在 C2 中单独处理）；(b) 含 α=0.60。两版对比本身就是有价值的叙事——模型在非 sync-dip 区域预测精确，在 sync-dip 区域系统性偏差。\n\n**陷阱 3：β 的处理方式需要一致**。当前 β=0.24 是从旧模型（P_max=1.0）固定的，在 P_max=0.75 下不可识别（ΔNLL<2 范围 β∈[0.05, 0.70]）。LOO-CV 中必须保持 β 在所有 fold 中固定为同一值（0.24），不能让每个 fold 自由拟合 β——否则 β 会在不同 fold 中取到很不同的值，产生虚假的良好拟合。**明确报告**：每个 fold 只拟合 2 个自由参数 (A, z₀)，P_max=0.75 和 β=0.24 均固定。\n\n**补充建议**：不仅做 LOO-CV，还要加 **模型比较**（Director 已提到 AIC/BIC）。具体对比模型：\n- M0: 常数模型（P=P_max 当 α>0.45）\n- M1: 线性 α_c(T) = a − bT\n- M2: 幂律 α_c(T) = A·T^{-γ}（当前模型）\n- M3: 指数 α_c(T) = A·exp(−bT)\n用 AIC 排序。如果 M2 的 AIC 显著最优，这本身就回应了\"零自由度\"质疑。\n\n**是否需要额外 T 值？** 不需要。5 个 T 值（覆盖 5× 范围）+ LOO-CV + 模型比较 足以回应。T=8/T=12 是锦上添花但时间成本高（需训练新实验），不如用好现有 20/20 数据。\n\n---\n\n**R8-C3 (Power artifact)**\n[severity: warning] **cross-T scaling consistency 必要但不充分。强烈建议加 formal simulation。**\n\n核心问题：如果真实信号随 α 平滑增长（无 phase transition），但 PermG 检验功效随 T 增加（更多时间点 → 更多统计功效），那\"观测到的 onset 随 T 左移\"**可以纯粹由功效增长解释**，无需假设真实 phase transition 存在。而且功效增长可能恰好近似 T^{-γ} 的 scaling。\n\n**建议方案**（计算成本约 1-2 小时 CPU，无需 GPU）：\n1. 设 H0：信号 = c·α（线性增长，无 transition），生成 1000 组合成时间序列\n2. 对每组施加 PermG 检验，找\"apparent onset\"（首次 FDR 显著的 α）\n3. 在不同 T 值下重复，看 apparent onset 是否遵循 T^{-γ}\n4. 如果遵循 → power artifact concern 成立，需要重构 framing\n5. 如果不遵循 → <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012FpWPXu4REE5h2esLzePW3`</summary>

```text
1	# Power Artifact Simulation Analysis
2	
3	## Purpose
4	
5	Reviewer W-C3 raised the concern that observed sharp onset α_c(T) ∝ T^{−2.08}
6	could be a statistical power artifact: if real signal grows smoothly with α,
7	increasing T simply raises PermG power, making the apparent onset shift left.
8	
9	This simulation tests H0 (linear signal, no transition) against observations.
10	
11	## Setup
12	
13	- Simulations per (α,T) cell: **1000**
14	- Permutations per test: **1000**
15	- Pairs per simulation: 8, FDR q=0.05
16	- VAR(1): a=0.5, c=0.5, σ=1.0
17	- Elapsed: 503.5s
18	
19	## Calibration
20	
21	Target: mean detection fraction = 0.75 at α=0.75, T=20 (matching observed 6/8).
22	
23	- **c_scale** = 1.3622 → H0 signal: S(α) = 1.3622 × α
24	- **S_max** = 1.0217 → H1 step amplitude
25	
26	## H0: Linear Signal (No Phase Transition)
27	
28	Under H0, signal S(α) = c × α increases monotonically. There is **no true
29	transition point** — detection onset is purely a statistical power artifact.
30	
31	### H0 Detection Rates P(PermG ≥ 1)
32	
33	| α \ T | 5 | 10 | 15 | 20 | 25 |
34	|------|------|------|------|------|------|
35	| 0.00 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.06 | 0.06 | 0.05 |
36	| 0.10 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.07 | 0.10 | 0.12 |
37	| 0.25 | 0.01 | 0.12 | 0.23 | 0.31 | 0.41 |
38	| 0.45 | 0.02 | 0.27 | 0.60 | 0.81 | 0.93 |
39	| 0.50 | 0.01 | 0.36 | 0.72 | 0.89 | 0.98 |
40	| 0.60 | 0.01 | 0.47 | 0.87 | 0.97 | 1.00 |
41	| 0.75 | 0.02 | 0.70 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
42	| 1.00 | 0.02 | 0.90 | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
43	
44	## H1: Step Transition (Phase Transition Model)
45	
46	Under H1, signal is a step function: S(α,T) = S_max × I(α ≥ α_c(T)),
47	where α_c(T) = 130.39 × T^{−2.083}.
48	
49	### H1 Detection Rates P(PermG ≥ 1)
50	
51	| α \ T | 5 | 10 | 15 | 20 | 25 |
52	|------|------|------|------|------|------|
53	| 0.00 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.06 | 0.05 | 0.05 |
54	| 0.10 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.04 | 0.07 | 0.06 |
55	| 0.25 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.05 | 0.05 | 1.00 |
56	| 0.45 | 0.01 | 0.06 | 0.06 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
57	| 0.50 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.98 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
58	| 0.60 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
59	| 0.75 | 0.02 | 0.05 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
60	| 1.00 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |
61	
62	## Apparent Onset Comparison
63	
64	Smallest α where P(PermG ≥ 1) > 0.50:
65	
66	| T | H0 | H1 | Theory α_c |
67	|---|------|------|------------|
68	| 5 | — | — | 4.5634 |
69	| 10 | 0.6199 | — | 1.0771 |
70	| 15 | 0.3945 | 0.474 | 0.4629 |
71	| 20 | 0.3257 | 0.3444 | 0.2542 |
72	| 25 | 0.2851 | 0.1701 | 0.1597 |
73	
74	## Scaling Fits
75	
76	- **H0**: α_c(T) ∝ T^{−0.848} (c=4.189, R²=0.977)
77	- **H1**: α_c(T) ∝ T^{−1.964} (c=104.224, R²=0.920)
78	- **Observed**: α_c(T) ∝ T^{−2.083} (c=130.390)
79	
80	## Conclusion
81	
82	**REAL_TRANSITION**
83	
84	H0 apparent onset scales as T^(-0.85), far from observed T^(-2.08) (Δγ=1.24 ≥ 0.5). Observed scaling cannot be explained by power artifact alone.
85	
86	### Interpretation
87	
88	The smooth-signal model (H0) produces an apparent onset pattern with a
89	fundamentally different scaling exponent than what we observe empirically.
90	A pure power-artifact explanation would predict γ ≈ 0.5 (from √T scaling
91	of F-test power), not the observed γ ≈ 2.08. This gap cannot be bridged
92	by adjusting the signal function — the power-law exponent is a structural
93	property of how statistical power scales with T, independent of signal shape.
94	
95	The observed steep scaling (γ ≈ 2.08) requires a mechanism beyond power
96	growth: a genuine phase transition in the signal structure.
97

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent. This is the power artifact simulation I recommended in Report #69. Let me analyze it critically.

## Key Results

**H0 (Linear signal, no transition):**
- Apparent onset: T=10→0.62, T=15→0.39, T=20→0.33, T=25→0.29
- Scaling: γ = 0.85, R² = 0.977

**H1 (Step transition):**
- Apparent onset: T=10→null, T=15→0.47, T=20→0.34, T=25→0.17


- Scaling: γ = 1.96, R² = 0.920 (only 3 points, T=10 had no onset)

**Observed:**
- α_c(T) ∝ T^{-2.083}

**Conclusion: "REAL_TRANSITION"** — H0 produces γ ≈ 0.85, far from observed γ ≈ 2.08.

The exponent difference of 1.24 between the two hypotheses is substantial and clearly separates them. The simulation appears well-calibrated so far, though I'm noticing the step transition model only fits three points since T=10 showed no onset.

The VAR(1) simulation is calibrated to match the observed detection rate at α=0.75 and T=20, which gives me confidence in the comparison. With 1000 simulations, there's good statistical power, and the null hypothesis appropriately produces around 5% false positive rates across all temperatures.

But here's where I need to be careful: the VAR(1) model with those simple parameters is quite simplified compared to the actual dynamics of entropy and diversity time series from iterative training. The real data likely has much more complex structure, so whether this simplified model can truly rule out power artifacts depends on how well it approximates the actual data-generating process.

That said, the core argument holds up structurally. The scaling exponent γ is fundamentally about how statistical power grows with sample size T, and for any reasonably smooth signal, that growth should follow roughly √T, giving γ ≈ 0.5. The observed γ ≈ 2.08 is four times larger than that, which is a huge discrepancy that's hard to explain away just by model mismatch.

The reviewer might push back on the linear signal assumption—what if the true relationship between α and the signal is exponential or some other highly nonlinear shape? That could make the critical threshold sharper and change how quickly detection drops with T. But the analysis already addresses this by arguing that the power-law exponent is a structural property that holds across different signal shapes.

Actually, I need to be more careful here. If the signal follows S(α) = c·α^k for some exponent k, then the critical threshold scales as α_c ∝ T^{-1/(2k)}, which means γ_H0 = 1/(2k). For a linear signal (k=1), that gives γ=0.5. For a quadratic signal (k=2), it drops to γ=0.25. More nonlinearity actually makes γ smaller, not larger. So the observed γ=2.08 is vastly larger than what any reasonable signal shape would predict, which actually strengthens the case that something structural is different about this problem.

The simulation gave γ=0.85 under the null, which is still far from 2.08. The gap comes from the complexity of the actual detection procedure—using FDR-corrected pairs and the VAR dynamics introduces temporal correlation that shifts things away from the simple theoretical prediction. But even accounting for that, 0.85 versus 2.08 is a huge discrepancy.

Now looking at the H1 fit itself, the power-law scaling gives γ=1.96 with R²=0.920, but the p-value is 0.18, which means this fit isn't statistically significant.

However, that's actually not surprising. The step-function H1 produces sharp transitions right at the critical threshold, so the apparent onset tracks the true α_c almost perfectly. The weak p-value is just because there are only three data points—T=15, 20, 25—since T=5 and T=10 showed no onset at all. It's a data scarcity issue, not a fit quality problem.

There's also a definitional mismatch I should flag: the analysis defines onset as the smallest α where P(PermG ≥ 1) exceeds 0.50, which is the median detection threshold. The paper's empirical α_c comes from a different definition based on the power theory fit. They're not quite the same, though the key thing is that both H0 and the observed exponents use the same definition, so they're at least comparable.

The simulation ran 1000 permutations per test versus 5000 in the real experiments, which could make p-values coarser and affect FDR precision. But the core finding—that H0 gives γ≈0.85 while observations show γ≈2.08—is so stark that more permutations wouldn't flip the conclusion. The evidence is strong that the observed scaling can't be a pure power artifact under the smooth-signal model, though I should acknowledge the VAR(1) is a simplification of the actual dynamics.

The H1 fit relies on just three data points and the "apparent onset" definition diverges from the paper's power-theory threshold, but the gap between the two scaling exponents is too large to dismiss as methodological noise. The simulation is well-designed and directly tackles the R8-C3 concern, so I'll endorse it while flagging these caveats. Now let me review the code for any bugs.
```

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""
3	Power Artifact Simulation — R8 W-C3 rebuttal.
4	Tests if observed α_c(T) ∝ T^{-2.08} is a genuine phase transition
5	or an artifact of PermG statistical power increasing with T.
6	
7	H0 (no transition): S(α) = c·α  (linear, all α>0 carry signal)
8	H1 (transition):    S(α,T) = S_max·I(α ≥ α_c(T))  (step function)
9	"""
10	
11	import numpy as np
12	from scipy import stats
13	from multiprocessing import Pool, cpu_count
14	import json, time, os, argparse
15	
16	# ── Configuration ──
17	ALPHAS = np.array([0.00, 0.10, 0.25, 0.45, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00])
18	T_VALUES = np.array([5, 10, 15, 20, 25])
19	N_PAIRS = 8
20	FDR_Q = 0.05
21	AR_A = 0.5
22	AR_C = 0.5
23	SIGMA = 1.0
24	GAMMA_OBS = 2.083
25	C_THEORY = 130.39
26	
27	
28	def alpha_c_theory(T):
29	    return C_THEORY * float(T) ** (-GAMMA_OBS)
30	
31	
32	# ── Core Simulation ──
33	
34	def gen_var1(n_total, T, b, rng):
35	    """Generate VAR(1) pairs, return first-differenced (dX, dY) each (n_total, T)."""
36	    X = np.zeros((n_total, T + 1))
37	    Y = np.zeros((n_total, T + 1))
38	    for t in range(T):
39	        X[:, t + 1] = AR_C * X[:, t] + rng.normal(0, SIGMA, n_total)
40	        Y[:, t + 1] = AR_A * Y[:, t] + b * X[:, t] + rng.normal(0, SIGMA, n_total)
41	    return np.diff(X, axis=1), np.diff(Y, axis=1)
42	
43	
44	def bh_fdr(pvals, q=FDR_Q):
45	    m = len(pvals)
46	    idx = np.argsort(pvals)
47	    sp = pvals[idx]
48	    thr = q * np.arange(1, m + 1) / m
49	    sig = np.zeros(m, dtype=bool)
50	    rej = sp <= thr
51	    if rej.any():
52	        sig[idx[: np.max(np.where(rej)[0]) + 1]] = True
53	    return sig
54	
55	
56	def permg_cell(dX, dY, n_sim, n_perm, rng):
57	    """Vectorized PermG across all n_sim*N_PAIRS pairs.
58	
59	    Uses partial-F formula (Frisch-Waugh-Lovell) to avoid redundant OLS per permutation.
60	    dX, dY: (n_sim * N_PAIRS, T)
61	    Returns: per-simulation PermG scores array (n_sim,)
62	    """
63	    n_total, T = dX.shape
64	    n = T - 1  # effective sample size after lag-1
65	    if n < 3:
66	        return np.zeros(n_sim)
67	
68	    dy = dY[:, 1:]       # response
69	    dy_lag = dY[:, :-1]   # lagged dy (restricted predictor)
70	    dx_lag = dX[:, :-1]   # lagged dx (tested predictor)
71	
72	    # Restricted OLS: dy ~ 1 + dy_lag (vectorized across pairs)
73	    s1 = dy_lag.sum(1)
74	    s2 = (dy_lag ** 2).sum(1)
75	    sy = dy.sum(1)
76	    sxy = (dy_lag * dy).sum(1)
77	    det = np.clip(n * s2 - s1 ** 2, 1e-12, None)
78	
79	    b0 = (s2 * sy - s1 * sxy) / det
80	    b1 = (n * sxy - s1 * sy) / det
81	    e_r = dy - b0[:, None] - b1[:, None] * dy_lag
82	    rss_r = (e_r ** 2).sum(1)
83	    df_u = n - 3
84	    if df_u <= 0:
85	        return np.zeros(n_sim)
86	
87	    def partial_f(z):
88	        """Partial F-stat for adding z to restricted model. z: (n_total, n)."""
89	        sz = z.sum(1)
90	        sxz = (dy_lag * z).sum(1)
91	        bz0 = (s2 * sz - s1 * sxz) / det
92	        bz1 = (n * sxz - s1 * sz) / det
93	        mr_z = z - bz0[:, None] - bz1[:, None] * dy_lag
94	        edz = (e_r * z).sum(1)
95	        zmz = np.clip((z * mr_z).sum(1), 1e-12, None)
96	        delta = edz ** 2 / zmz
97	        rss_u = np.clip(rss_r - delta, 1e-12, None)
98	        return np.clip(delta * df_u / rss_u, 0, None)
99	
100	    F_orig = partial_f(dx_lag)
101	
102	    count_ge = np.zeros(n_total)
103	    for _ in range(n_perm):
104	        dx_perm = rng.permuted(dX, axis=1)
105	        F_p = partial_f(dx_perm[:, :-1])
106	        count_ge += (F_p >= F_orig)
107	
108	    pvals = (count_ge + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
109	    pv_mat = pvals.reshape(n_sim, N_PAIRS)
110	    scores = np.array([bh_fdr(pv_mat[i]).sum() for i in range(n_sim)])
111	    return scores
112	
113	
114	def run_cell(args):
115	    hyp, alpha, T, n_sim, n_perm, b, seed = args
116	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
117	    dX, dY = gen_var1(n_sim * N_PAIRS, T, b, rng)
118	    sc = permg_cell(dX, dY, n_sim, n_perm, rng)
119	    return {
120	        "hypothesis": hyp,
121	        "alpha": float(alpha),
122	        "T": int(T),
123	        "b": float(b),
124	        "mean_permg_score": round(float(sc.mean()), 4),
125	        "detection_rate": round(float((sc >= 1).mean()), 4),
126	        "mean_detection_fraction": round(float((sc / N_PAIRS).mean()), 4),
127	    }
128	
129	
130	# ── Calibration ──
131	
132	def calibrate(target_frac, T, n_sim=100, n_perm=200):
133	    """Binary search for VAR coefficient b giving target mean detection fraction."""
134	    lo, hi = 0.05, 10.0
135	    for i in range(15):
136	        b = (lo + hi) / 2
137	        rng = np.random.default_rng(42)  # fixed seed for monotonic convergence
138	        dX, dY = gen_var1(n_sim * N_PAIRS, T, b, rng)
139	        sc = permg_cell(dX, dY, n_sim, n_perm, rng)
140	        frac = (sc / N_PAIRS).mean()
141	        print(f"  calib {i:2d}: b={b:.4f}  frac={frac:.3f}  target={target_frac:.3f}")
142	        if abs(frac - target_frac) < 0.02:
143	            return b
144	        if frac < target_frac:
145	            lo = b
146	        else:
147	            hi = b
148	    return (lo + hi) / 2
149

151	# ── Analysis ──
152	
153	def find_onset(results, thr=0.5):
154	    """Find onset α via linear interpolation where detection_rate crosses threshold."""
155	    out = {}
156	    for T in T_VALUES:
157	        cells = sorted(
158	            [(r["alpha"], r["detection_rate"]) for r in results if r["T"] == T]
159	        )
160	        onset = None
161	        for i in range(len(cells)):
162	            a, dr = cells[i]
163	            if dr > thr:
164	                if i == 0:
165	                    onset = a
166	                else:
167	                    a_prev, dr_prev = cells[i - 1]
168	                    span = dr - dr_prev
169	                    if span > 0:
170	                        onset = a_prev + (thr - dr_prev) / span * (a - a_prev)
171	                    else:
172	                        onset = a
173	                break
174	        out[int(T)] = round(onset, 4) if onset is not None else None
175	    return out
176

178	def fit_powerlaw(onsets):
179	    pts = [(T, a) for T, a in onsets.items() if a is not None and a > 0]
180	    if len(pts) < 2:
181	        return None
182	    Ts, As = zip(*pts)
183	    sl, ic, r, p, se = stats.linregress(np.log(Ts), np.log(As))
184	    return {
185	        "c": round(float(np.exp(ic)), 4),
186	        "gamma": round(float(-sl), 4),
187	        "r2": round(float(r ** 2), 4),
188	        "p": round(float(p), 6),
189	    }
190	
191

212	
213	    # Build task list (H0 + H1 × α × T)
214	    tasks = []
215	    seed = 314159

      "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nPower Artifact Simulation — R8 W-C3 rebuttal.\nTests if observed α_c(T) ∝ T^{-2.08} is a genuine phase transition\nor an artifact of PermG statistical power increasing with T.\n\nH0 (no transition): S(α) = c·α  (linear, all α>0 carry signal)\nH1 (transition):    S(α,T) = S_max·I(α ≥ α_c(T))  (step function)\n\"\"\"\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import stats\nfrom multiprocessing import Pool, cpu_count\nimport json, time, os, argparse\n\n# ── Configuration ──\nALPHAS = np.array([0.00, 0.10, 0.25, 0.45, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00])\nT_VALUES = np.array([5, 10, 15, 20, 25])\nN_PAIRS = 8\nFDR_Q = 0.05\nAR_A = 0.5\nAR_C = 0.5\nSIGMA = 1.0\nGAMMA_OBS = 2.083\nC_THEORY = 130.39\n\n\ndef alpha_c_theory(T):\n    return C_THEORY * float(T) ** (-GAMMA_OBS)\n\n\n# ── Core Simulation ──\n\ndef gen_var1(n_total, T, b, rng):\n    \"\"\"Generate VAR(1) pairs, return first-differenced (dX, dY) each (n_total, T).\"\"\"\n    X = np.zeros((n_total, T + 1))\n    Y = np.zeros((n_total, T + 1))\n    for t in range(T):\n        X[:, t + 1] = AR_C * X[:, t] + rng.normal(0, SIGMA, n_total)\n        Y[:, t + 1] = AR_A * Y[:, t] + b * X[:, t] + rng.normal(0, SIGMA, n_total)\n    return np.diff(X, axis=1), np.diff(Y, axis=1)\n\n\ndef bh_fdr(pvals, q=FDR_Q):\n    m = len(pvals)\n    idx = np.argsort(pvals)\n    sp = pvals[idx]\n    thr = q * np.arange(1, m + 1) / m\n    sig = np.zeros(m, dtype=bool)\n    rej = sp <= thr\n    if rej.any():\n        sig[idx[: np.max(np.where(rej)[0]) + 1]] = True\n    return sig\n\n\ndef permg_cell(dX, dY, n_sim, n_perm, rng):\n    \"\"\"Vectorized PermG across all n_sim*N_PAIRS pairs.\n\n    Uses partial-F formula (Frisch-Waugh-Lovell) to avoid redundant OLS per permutation.\n    dX, dY: (n_sim * N_PAIRS, T)\n    Returns: per-simulation PermG scores array (n_sim,)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_total, T = dX.shape\n    n = T - 1  # effective sample size after lag-1\n    if n < 3:\n        return np.zeros(n_sim)\n\n    dy = dY[:, 1:]       # response\n    dy_lag = dY[:, :-1]   # lagged dy (restricted predictor)\n    dx_lag = dX[:, :-1]   # lagged dx (tested predictor)\n\n    # Restricted OLS: dy ~ 1 + dy_lag (vectorized across pairs)\n    s1 = dy_lag.sum(1)\n    s2 = (dy_lag ** 2).sum(1)\n    sy = dy.sum(1)\n    sxy = (dy_lag * dy).sum(1)\n    det = np.clip(n * s2 - s1 ** 2, 1e-12, None)\n\n    b0 = (s2 * sy - s1 * sxy) / det\n    b1 = (n * sxy - s1 * sy) / det\n    e_r = dy - b0[:, None] - b1[:, None] * dy_lag\n    rss_r = (e_r ** 2).sum(1)\n    df_u = n - 3\n    if df_u <= 0:\n        return np.zeros(n_sim)\n\n    def partial_f(z):\n        \"\"\"Partial F-stat for adding z to restricted model. z: (n_total, n).\"\"\"\n        sz = z.sum(1)\n        sxz = (dy_lag * z).sum(1)\n        bz0 = (s2 * sz - s1 * sxz) / det\n        bz1 = (n * sxz - s1 * sz) / det\n        mr_z = z - bz0[:, None] - bz1[:, None] * dy_lag\n        edz = (e_r * z).sum(1)\n        zmz = np.clip((z * mr_z).sum(1), 1e-12, None)\n        delta = edz ** 2 / zmz\n        rss_u = np.clip(rss_r - delta, 1e-12, None)\n        return np.clip(delta * df_u / rss_u, 0, None)\n\n    F_orig = partial_f(dx_lag)\n\n    count_ge = np.zeros(n_total)\n    for _ in range(n_perm):\n        dx_perm = rng.permuted(dX, axis=1)\n        F_p = partial_f(dx_perm[:, :-1])\n        count_ge += (F_p >= F_orig)\n\n    pvals = (count_ge + 1) / (n_perm + 1)\n    pv_mat = pvals.reshape(n_sim, N_PAIRS)\n    scores = np.array([bh_fdr(pv_mat[i]).sum() for i in range(n_sim)])\n    return scores\n\n\ndef run_cell(args):\n    hyp, alpha, T, n_sim, n_perm, b, seed = args\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    dX, dY = gen_var1(n_sim * N_PAIRS, T, b, rng)\n    sc = permg_cell(dX, dY, n_sim, n_perm, rng)\n    return {\n        \"hypothesis\": hyp,\n        \"alpha\": float(alpha),\n        \"T\": int(T),\n        \"b\": float(b),\n        \"mean_permg_score\": round(float(sc.mean()), 4),\n        \"detection_rate\": round(float((sc >= 1).mean()), 4),\n        \"mean_detection_fraction\": round(float((sc / N_PAIRS).mean()), 4),\n    }\n\n\n# ── Calibration ──\n\ndef calibrate(target_frac, T, n_sim=100, n_perm=200):\n    \"\"\"Binary search for VAR coefficient b giving target mean detection fraction.\"\"\"\n    lo, hi = 0.05, 10.0\n    for i in range(15):\n        b = (lo + hi) / 2\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(42)  # fixed seed for monotonic convergence\n        dX, dY = gen_var1(n_sim * N_PAIRS, T, b, rng)\n        sc = permg_cell(dX, dY, n_sim, n_perm, rng)\n        frac = (sc / N_PAIRS).mean()\n        print(f\"  calib {i:2d}: b={b:.4f}  frac={frac:.3f}  target={target_frac:.3f}\")\n        if abs(frac - target_frac) < 0.02:\n            return b\n        if frac < target_frac:\n            lo = b\n        else:\n            hi = b\n    return (lo + hi) / 2\n\n\n# ── Analysis ──\n\ndef find_onset(results, thr=0.5):\n    \"\"\"Find onset α via linear interpolation where detection_rate crosses threshold.\"\"\"\n    out = {}\n    for T in T_VALUES:\n        cells = sorted(\n            [(r[\"alpha\"], r[\"detection_rate\"]) for r in results if r[\"T\"] == T]\n        )\n        onset = None\n        for i in range(len(cells)):\n            a, dr = cells[i]\n            if dr > thr:\n                if i == 0:\n                    onset = a\n                else:\n                    a_prev, dr_prev = cells[i - 1]\n                    span = dr - dr_prev\n                    if span > 0:\n                        onset = a_prev + (thr - dr_prev) / span * (a - a_prev)\n                    else:\n                        onset = a\n                break\n        out[int(T)] = round(onset, 4) if onset is not None else None\n    return out\n\n\ndef fit_powerlaw(onsets):\n    pts = [(T, a) for T, a in onsets.items() if a is not None and a > 0]\n    if len(pts) < 2:\n        return None\n    Ts, As = zip(*pts)\n    sl, ic, r, p, se = stats.linregress(np.log(Ts), np.log(As))\n    return {\n        \"c\": round(float(np.exp(ic)), 4),\n        \"gamma\": round(float(-sl), 4),\n        \"r2\": round(float(r ** 2), 4),\n        \"p\": round(float(p), 6),\n    }\n\n\n# ── Main ──\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--mode\", default=\"full\", choices=[\"pilot\", \"full\"])\n # Build task list (H0 + H1 × α × T)\n    tasks = []\n    seed = 314159\n    for ia, a in enumerate(ALPHAS):\n        for it, T in enumerate(T_VALUES):\n            idx = ia * len(T_VALUES) + it\n            tasks.append((\"H0\", a, T, n_sim, n_perm, c_scale * a, seed + idx))\n            ac = alpha_c_theory(T)\n            b_h1 = S_max if a >= ac else 0.0\n            tasks.append(\n                (\"H1\", a, T, n_sim, n_perm, b_h1, seed + 10000 + idx)\n            )\n\n    print(f\"--- Running {len(tasks)} cells ---\")\n    t0 = time.time()\n    nw = min(cpu_count(), 16)\n    with Pool(nw) as increases monotonically. ```text
I've now reviewed the full simulation code. Let me provide a thorough critical assessment.

## Code Review

### Strengths:
1. **Clean implementation**: VAR(1) DGP, vectorized PermG with Frisch-Waugh-Lovell partial F, proper BH-FDR correction
2. **Calibration**: Binary search for coupling coefficient b at (α=0.75, T=20) → target 0.75 detection fraction. Ensures fair comparison with empirical data.
3. **Dual hypothesis**: H0 (linear signal) and H1 (step transition) both tested


4. **Onset detection**: Linear interpolation for P(detect) > 0.50 threshold
5. **Parallelized**: multiprocessing Pool for the 80 cells

### Issues I need to flag:

**Issue 1: VAR(1) DGP is simplified**
The actual PermG test in the paper operates on time series of (entropy, distinct, mauve, ece) across T generations. The simulation uses a simple VAR(1) with Gaussian innovations, but real data exhibits non-Gaussian distributions, potentially nonlinear dynamics, heteroscedasticity, and much shorter time series (T=5 to T=20).

However, the core insight — that power scaling exponent is a structural property — is robust to DGP details. The simulation correctly captures the essential features: lagged dependence, noise, and the FDR-corrected permutation test.

**Issue 2: H1 uses a step function, but real onset is gradual**
The H1 model applies b=S_max for α ≥ α_c(T) and b=0 otherwise, creating a perfectly sharp transition. In reality, the transition is gradual (sigmoid-like, per the P_max model), which would make H1 less distinguishable from H0.

However, the key comparison is H0 vs observed, not H0 vs H1. The H1 is just a reference showing that a genuine transition produces γ close to the observed value.

**Issue 3: Calibration uses fixed seed**
The calibrate() function locks in a seed for reproducible convergence, which is fine for calibration but means the calibrated b value depends on that seed. The main simulation correctly varies seeds across cells.

**Issue 4: Onset definition uses 50% detection threshold**
The paper measures onset as P(PermG_forward_FDR > 0), while the simulation defines it as the 50% crossing of detection_rate — the fraction of simulations with FDR-significant forward pairs. This is appropriate but worth noting.

**Issue 5: Permutation count trade-off**
The simulation uses 1000 permutations per test for computational feasibility, though empirical validation might benefit from 5000.  Fewer permutations make p-values coarser and could affect FDR correction, but 1000 is standard practice and unlikely to change the qualitative findings.

**Issue 6: Theoretical γ vs empirical γ**
The analysis claims γ ≈ 0.5 from √T scaling, but that's oversimplified. The simulation achieves γ=0.85, which exceeds the theoretical 0.5 because FDR correction, multiple testing across 8 pairs, and VAR dynamics all push the exponent higher. Still, 0.85 remains well below the 2.08 threshold. There's a subtlety in the theoretical argument under H0 that I'm working through — the detection scaling depends on how the signal strength grows with α.

The gap between theory (0.5) and simulation (0.85) likely stems from the permutation test having different power characteristics than a standard F-test, FDR correction modifying the detection threshold nonlinearly across the 8 pairs, and the VAR(1) lag structure affecting the effective sample size.

But even at 0.85, this is still far below 2.08, so the core argument remains intact. The claim that the exponent is independent of signal shape needs refinement though — the exponent actually depends on how the signal scales with effect size. For a signal that grows as α^k, the exponent is 1/(2k), so a weakly concave signal could push it toward 1.0, but reaching 2.08 would require an extremely weak scaling like α^0.24, which seems implausible without very specific contamination patterns.

The simulation itself is solid and the conclusion holds up: the observed gap of 1.24 between the theoretical and empirical exponents is large enough to rule out pure power-law artifacts. I should present the endorsement of the main finding while flagging these methodological nuances.
```

   - **α_c(T) = 59.01·T^(-2.083)**: Phase boundary formula from P_max model

{
  "parentUuid": "74936a76-26fc-47e1-b18a-855c6b8a012a",

  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/power_simulation/power_artifact_analysis.md",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/power_simulation/power_artifact_analysis.md",
        "content": "# Power Artifact Simulation Analysis\n\n## Purpose\n\nReviewer W-C3 raised the concern that observed sharp onset α_c(T) ∝ T^{−2.08}\ncould be a statistical power artifact: if real signal grows smoothly with α,\nincreasing T simply raises PermG power, making the apparent onset shift left.\n\nThis simulation tests H0 (linear signal, no transition) against observations.\n\n## Setup\n\n- Simulations per (α,T) cell: **1000**\n- Permutations per test: **1000**\n- Pairs per simulation: 8, FDR q=0.05\n- VAR(1): a=0.5, c=0.5, σ=1.0\n- Elapsed: 503.5s\n\n## Calibration\n\nTarget: mean detection fraction = 0.75 at α=0.75, T=20 (matching observed 6/8).\n\n- **c_scale** = 1.3622 → H0 signal: S(α) = 1.3622 × α\n- **S_max** = 1.0217 → H1 step amplitude\n\n## H0: Linear Signal (No Phase Transition)\n\nUnder H0, signal S(α) = c × α increases monotonically. There is **no true\ntransition point** — detection onset is purely a statistical power artifact.\n\n### H0 Detection Rates P(PermG ≥ 1)\n\n| α \\ T | 5 | 10 | 15 | 20 | 25 |\n|------|------|------|------|------|------|\n| 0.00 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.06 | 0.06 | 0.05 |\n| 0.10 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.07 | 0.10 | 0.12 |\n| 0.25 | 0.01 | 0.12 | 0.23 | 0.31 | 0.41 |\n| 0.45 | 0.02 | 0.27 | 0.60 | 0.81 | 0.93 |\n| 0.50 | 0.01 | 0.36 | 0.72 | 0.89 | 0.98 |\n| 0.60 | 0.01 | 0.47 | 0.87 | 0.97 | 1.00 |\n| 0.75 | 0.02 | 0.70 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n| 1.00 | 0.02 | 0.90 | 1.00 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n\n## H1: Step Transition (Phase Transition Model)\n\nUnder H1, signal is a step function: S(α,T) = S_max × I(α ≥ α_c(T)),\nwhere α_c(T) = 130.39 × T^{−2.083}.\n\n### H1 Detection Rates P(PermG ≥ 1)\n\n| α \\ T | 5 | 10 | 15 | 20 | 25 |\n|------|------|------|------|------|------|\n| 0.00 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.06 | 0.05 | 0.05 |\n| 0.10 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.04 | 0.07 | 0.06 |\n| 0.25 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.05 | 0.05 | 1.00 |\n| 0.45 | 0.01 | 0.06 | 0.06 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n| 0.50 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.98 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n| 0.60 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n| 0.75 | 0.02 | 0.05 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n| 1.00 | 0.01 | 0.05 | 0.97 | 1.00 | 1.00 |\n\n## Apparent Onset Comparison\n\nSmallest α where P(PermG ≥ 1) > 0.50:\n\n| T | H0 | H1 | Theory α_c |\n|---|------|------|------------|\n| 5 | — | — | 4.5634 |\n| 10 | 0.6199 | — | 1.0771 |\n| 15 | 0.3945 | 0.474 | 0.4629 |\n| 20 | 0.3257 | 0.3444 | 0.2542 |\n| 25 | 0.2851 | 0.1701 | 0.1597 |\n\n## Scaling Fits\n\n- **H0**: α_c(T) ∝ T^{−0.848} (c=4.189, R²=0.977)\n- **H1**: α_c(T) ∝ T^{−1.964} (c=104.224, R²=0.920)\n- **Observed**: α_c(T) ∝ T^{−2.083} (c=130.390)\n\n## Conclusion\n\n**REAL_TRANSITION**\n\nH0 apparent onset scales as T^(-0.85), far from observed T^(-2.08) (Δγ=1.24 ≥ 0.5). Observed scaling cannot be explained by power artifact alone.\n\n### Interpretation\n\nThe smooth-signal model (H0) produces an apparent onset pattern with a\nfundamentally different scaling exponent than what we observe empirically.\nA pure power-artifact explanation would predict γ ≈ 0.5 (from √T scaling\nof F-test power), not the observed γ ≈ 2.08. This gap cannot be bridged\nby adjusting the signal function — the power-law exponent is a structural\nproperty of how statistical power scales with T, independent of signal shape.\n\nThe observed steep scaling (γ ≈ 2.08) requires a mechanism beyond power\ngrowth: a genuine phase transition in the signal structure.\n",
        "numLines": 97,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 97
      }
